ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u20	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I would first like to say that I hear unnecessary anxiety here in these questions, and I hope that the answer will calm the Members. In 2016 adopted by the Polish Sejm on 25 February 2016 in the special reserve under the name: Against the effects of natural disasters, financial resources of PLN 1 billion were included. It should be noted here that the previous government, the PO-PSL coalition, proposed in the draft budget law for 2016 the amount of this reserve of PLN 800 million. So our amendment made it 200 million more. This budget law adopted by the High House for 2016 containing higher than previously planned, as I said, measures for action related to disaster response enabled the granting of significant, although of course too small in relation to needs, but significant assistance to local government units, including Lower Silesia or Silesia. This year's funding is mainly devoted to flood protection projects of the rivers Oder and Vistula, reconstruction of municipal infrastructure of local authorities, tasks related to countering the effects of landslides movements and removing them, protection of Less gorges and reconstruction of flood infrastructure. These are the types of expenditure that are financed from this reserve. Ladies and gentlemen, as soon as the President of the Republic of Poland signed the 2016 Budget Act on 3 March 2016, the planned expenditure and the planned actions were determined. The Minister of Home Affairs and Administration also approved on the same day on 3 March the plan for the allocation of the special reserve: Anti-action and elimination of the effects of natural disasters, of which he is the authorising officer. Given the increased resources of the special-purpose reserve and the lines of expenditure already planned, the voivodships were required to urgently analyse the main needs for the elimination of damage to local infrastructure and to present proposals for specific tasks to be carried out this year. Within the limits allocated, individual voivodships, acting in agreement with local governments, proposed lists of priority tasks to be carried out this year together with the expected subsidies from the state budget. In view of the above actions taken since the beginning of April local governments in individual voivodships, including the local governments of the voivodships referred to by the Members, receive information about the financial assistance granted for the current year to remedy the consequences of natural disasters. In 2016, the special reserve of the State budget for the prevention and disposal of natural disasters, as planned, for local government units to carry out the tasks reported as the most urgent to carry out the renovation or reconstruction of the municipal infrastructure destroyed as a result of natural disasters, amounted to PLN 150 million. The procedure for granting budgetary aid to self-government units has not changed since last year, that is, honourable Members, the same algorithm that was used in previous years under the PO-PSL regime. [[Then why do we have less?]] Reported by... Of course, it can be discussed about its change, but for this year it has been applied in the unchanged formula. The objects reported by the voivodships were damaged or destroyed in previous years by natural disasters occurring throughout the country. In accordance with approved on 16 March this year. by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration, the distribution of funds to local government units from the area of the Lower Silesian Voivodeship was allocated - because here at the beginning there was a question about Dolny Śląsk - the amount of PLN 31 million. With regard to the amount and the Silesian voivodeship that you referred to, I must check whether it really is. Of course, I have all the attachments, I have details if necessary. In calculating the amount above, account was taken of the losses incurred in the voivodships in the years 2010 to 2015 and the sum of the subsidies already received in previous years. Here's one sentence of comment. If any of the voivodships had more funding in previous years, it could happen that they now receive less funding, and that is also understandable, within this algorithm and those criteria that I invoke. Following an analysis of the documents submitted and a finding of compliance of the notification with the requirements described in the current guidelines of the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration on the principles and modalities of granting special-purpose grants to local government units from the State budget for the financing of their own tasks related to the renovation and reconstruction of construction works destroyed or damaged as a result of natural disaster events, including the prevention of the effects of such events in the future - that is the long name of these guidelines - the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration 4 April this year. signed 46 promes for the requested by the Voivode of Lower Silesian units to carry out the tasks indicated in the requested request. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u30	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! Dear Minister, A lot has been said recently about rheumatic diseases that affect not only older people but also young people and children. Inflammatory rheumatic diseases include those in which the joints are destroyed as a result of chronic inflammatory processes in these structures. Treatment of these diseases is aimed at preventing and controlling joint damage, preventing loss of joint function, eliminating or reducing pain, seeking to achieve complete remission of the disease and, as a result, stopping disability and improving the quality of life of the patient. It is worrying that the number of public rheumatology clinics is decreasing. In 2010, according to the information we reached, there were about 928 clinics in all voivodships, today there are about 695, and that means that over 230 clinics disappeared over 5 years. Therefore, we ask the Minister whether he sees a risk associated with a small number of rheumatologists and public rheumatology institutions disappearing. Does this situation not translate negatively into early diagnosis of patients and thus, the implementation of treatment in the so-called. a therapeutic window that can have better effects for the patient?
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u31	Rheumaological diseases also affect the youngest group of patients, i.e. children. Therefore, I would like to ask you whether, in your opinion, the current number of children's rheumatologists is sufficient and whether it ensures the safety of the treatment of already ill and treating children. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u32	Thank you very much. The question is answered by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health, Mr Piotr Warczyński. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u33	Mrs. Marshal! Dear Members, Touching the problem of rheumatology, rheumatologists and rheumatology clinics, you have touched a very important problem of the number of doctors in Poland. As far as rheumatologists are concerned, we know that there are not enough of them at the moment. The consequence of a small number of rheumatologists is, of course, a reduction in the number of rheumatology clinics, because in these clinics specialists must take them. This problem concerns not only rheumatology, but also at least a dozen other specialties. The Minister of Health has been... The predecessors of the current Minister of Health have also implemented certain measures to prevent the reduction of the number of doctors. However, as we look at the process of education, starting with medical studies that last six years, one year of internship, or seven years, and a minimum of five years of specialization, the results are only visible after 12 years. That is why this problem will continue to function for several years. However, it is not so that at the moment the number of specialists in Poland is decreasing or the number of doctors in Poland is decreasing. We have a steady, slow, but steady increase in the number of doctors. When it comes to rheumatologists, it's similar. And now, in order to look comprehensively at the problems that you have signaled, you need to look at it from the very beginning, which is to start by analysing whether there is a real shortage of doctors and where these doctors are missing. I wanted to refer here to what is currently happening in the Ministry of Health, i.e. the development of maps of health needs, which show us two things, first of all two things, that is the state in which we really have in each voivodeship, in each county the structure of health protection, including medical personnel, and at the same time determining health needs, tell us in which direction we should make changes in these regions. The maps are now in an external consultation, they have been developed, so that by the end of April, final versions will be available in June, and I think we will then start a rather important discussion on what is changing in the regions. On the other hand, these maps provide information on how many doctors lack specialties in a given region. This will be very, very important information. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u50	Thank you very much. And I will ask for an answer from Minister Radosław Domagalski-Łabędzki. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u51	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to thank the Member for his interest in negotiating an agreement on a transatlantic trade and investment partnership between the European Union and the United States. The TTIP is a free trade agreement aimed at creating better conditions for the functioning of the two largest economies of the world by liberalising trade between the European Union and the United States. This liberalisation, if the agreement enters into force, will concern trade in goods, services and investment flows. Most general analyses indicate that the agreement will benefit the European Union and Poland in terms of growth in trade and GDP growth. For Poland, the projected GDP growth as a result of trade liberalisation resulting from TTIP over several years is estimated at 0,2%. , although there are also analyses which indicate that the benefits of GDP growth will be even greater, up to 0.5%. Export growth is on average around 30% - the increase in exports from Poland to the United States is about 30% - but we must remember that we are starting from a very low level, because direct exports from Poland to the United States are only 2,3% all of our exports. With regard to trade in goods, liberalisation will be implemented through the elimination of customs duties and so-called. non-tariff barriers. In accordance with the negotiating mandate, the main objective of the negotiations is to eliminate all customs duties, but for sensitive goods the mandate gives the possibility of special treatment and withdrawal from the principle of full liberalisation. During the negotiations, discussions on the liberalisation of trade in goods are conducted on the basis of tariff offers from parties where the European Union and the United States make proposals for liberalisation on their side and inform about expectations of the partners' proposals. So far, there have been two exchanges of offers. The last exchange took place in October last year. According to these offers, the reciprocal elimination of tariffs is expected to concern 97% tariff lines, including liberalisation on the date of entry into force of the Agreement, are foreseen for 87.5% products, tariff lines. For the others, transitional periods, up to a maximum of 10 years after the entry into force of the Agreement, and incomplete liberalisation shall be considered. Further negotiations will lead to a narrow list of goods. Directly referring to the question raised in relation to the mining and energy sector, it should be noted that, as regards import duties, TTIP remains, as a general rule, neutral because the current duty rates are zero for energy raw materials, including coal, gas or oil. At the same time, TTIP offers an opportunity to improve access to American shale gas. Today, regulatory barriers on the US market restrict or prevent gas exports, which are much cheaper than in Europe. And here an important note: Poland's position is to include a separate chapter devoted to energy issues. It is known that the United States prefers those partners who are covered by zones or free trade agreements. We want an appropriate record, also concerning access to energy carriers such as gas, to be expressis verbis expressed in these records, although this is the subject of negotiations that are ongoing. Overall, removing these barriers and importing gas would improve the competitiveness of energy-intensive sectors in Europe, including Poland. An example of such an energy-intensive sector in Poland is the chemical sector, where energy costs represent more than 60% of production costs for certain goods. Today, these differences in gas prices between the United States and the EU countries give a significant competitive advantage to US operators. Therefore, the European Union, at the request of Poland, treats the chemical sector as sensitive in the negotiations and this is the position presented by our team at the Commission. As far as the agri-food sector is concerned - and here the Member pointed out the characteristics of the American industry - it is true that there is a competitive advantage on the part of American producers in certain agricultural sectors, in the case of meat or cereals, there is indeed a powerful effect on a scale, which gives them a significant competitive advantage. Therefore, full liberalisation on the part of the European Union would most likely adversely affect the situation of agricultural producers, including of course in Poland. In addition, the level of customs protection in the European Union is significantly higher than in the United States, resulting in more benefits for the US side in the event of full liberalisation. Therefore, goods from the agricultural sector are considered particularly sensitive in TTIP negotiations. The outcome of the negotiations in this sector should take into account the possibility of partially excluding the most sensitive goods from the liberalisation of trade with the USA in the form of the application of tariff quotas or their total exclusion from liberalisation. Current status of tariff liberalisation negotiations [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the agricultural sector it largely fulfils these conditions. In addition, we aim to ensure that the TTIP agreement is equipped with an effective safeguard clause to protect markets in a situation of imbalance with excessive imports...
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u52	Minister, why don't we finish the answer after the next question, you'll have time for the full answer. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u92	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for that question. Indeed, the issue of oil and gas stocks is a strategic issue, and we have a number of different actions in relation to it, in order for us, as Poland, to be sufficiently equipped for those situations which you have been gracious to describe. Firstly, with regard to oil and fuel intervention stocks, the obligation is indeed 90 days, and at this point we have enough of these stocks to cover fuel demand over a period of 104 days. In addition, individual companies maintain their own operational and commercial stocks. In such a situation, if there was a situation that the Member described, these stocks, which are operational and commercial stocks, would be mobilised at the first moment. Moreover, both traders, who in the previous calendar year brought over 100,000. T crude oil or fuels, as well as producers and operators providing storage and/or transmission services for intervention stocks, are required to develop and update the procedures applicable in the event of disruptions, inter alia. those which you have described, i.e. disruptions in the import of crude oil or fuels, disruptions in the functioning of the oil infrastructure, as well as failures in the transmission, processing or storage system. By responding precisely to questions concerning IKS Solino, I think we should remember that when it comes to agency supplies, their availability is ensured by contract. Five days after the government's signal, we have 30 days to access these supplies. However, it must be remembered that IKS Solino is not the only place where intervention supplies or agency supplies are held, but we also have overhead warehouses, where there are about 50% of them. supplies. Therefore, in such a situation, when these stocks were launched on the part of the operating and commercial companies, these stocks would be mobilised in the vessels to which we have access. It should also be borne in mind that Poland, as a member of the European Union and of the international energy agency, participates in a collective, joint intervention mechanism in the oil market where Member States support each other in a situation of disruption in the supply of raw material to the market. I think it is also worth adding that the existing oil infrastructure in Poland allows imports into Polish oil refineries from different directions. Therefore, this situation described by your Member in the event of disturbances from one direction, in this case the eastern one, is also the subject of work. In recent years, Polish refineries have identified more than 100 different oil species, and thanks to the testing of these other oil species they are prepared to replace the Urals oil type. Last time information: in April 2016 the oil terminal of PERN m3. On the other hand, with regard to the second fuel, gas, one of the main elements of the security policy in the natural gas sector is, of course, the development of storage capacity. At present, in 2015 the active capacity of underground high methane gas storage facilities was 2.8 billion m3, i.e. Okay. 18% annual consumption, 25% of annual imports to Poland and one company that maintained mandatory stocks in terms of gas, i.e. PGNiG maintained these stocks of more than 800 million m3. Moreover, with regard to security of gas supply, measures are being taken to diversify supply and build new gas connections. This is primarily a matter of Baltic Pipe, which is the physical connection between northern Poland and the Norwegian shelf and thus provide us with alternative access to gas from a different direction than the eastern one, and that is to provide this access physically. We also have the possibility of reverse deliveries on the Jamał - Europa pipeline at the Mallnow point. This investment in the event of a crisis makes it possible to reverse the gas flow and make full use of this Mallnow point, i.e. 5.4 billion m3. I think as far as this initial information is concerned, your marshal, that's it. I'm at your disposal.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u228	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill amending the Labour Code Act and some other laws contained in Seym Print No. 382. Draft law amending the Labour Code Act and certain other laws amending the article. 176 The Labour Code was drawn up to ensure the correct transposition of the provisions of the 2006 Directive. of the European Parliament and of the Council of 5 July 2006 on the implementation of the principle of equal opportunities and equal treatment of men and women in the field of employment and occupation, and with a view to adapting the authorisation to the requirements of Article. 92 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Work on the project started in 2014. The project was then in inter-ministerial arrangements, public consultation. However, the previous Parliament has not adopted solutions, which is why this project is being presented again today. He also passed the inter-ministerial arrangements. At the moment, it is still being examined within the Social Dialogue Council. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! According to the current wording of the article. 176 The Labour Code must not employ women in work that is particularly burdensome or harmful to health. In that provision, the Council of Ministers has been empowered to establish a list of such work by means of a regulation. From the sound of the article. 176 The Labour Code shows that it applies to all women, not just pregnant and breastfeeding women. On the basis of this provision, it would therefore be possible to introduce far-reaching restrictions on the employment of all women, which, according to the European Commission, constitutes an obstacle to gender equality. The European Commission has expressed its opinion that the general prohibition on women's employment in work which is particularly burdensome or harmful to health is not justified in the context of EU rules on equal treatment between men and women in terms of access to employment, vocational training and promotion and working conditions. In the Commission's view, there is no reason to believe that health-related work poses a particular risk to women's health and that it is more harmful to women than to men. A similar argument applies to particularly burdensome work. The European Commission has pointed out that, according to the case law of the European Court of Justice, women must not be excluded from specific work on the grounds that public opinion will require stronger protection for men against the risks which affect men and women to the same extent and which do not relate to the specific protection requirements for women, in particular in relation to pregnancy and maternity. In general, the exclusion of women from certain positions due to a general threat which is not specific to women does not fall within the limits of permitted differences in the treatment of women and men in employment. The draft law put forward by the government explicitly includes the above ban on pregnant and breast-feeding women. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In the designed § Article 1 176 The Labour Code states that pregnant women and breast-feeding women will not be able to carry out work that is burdensome, dangerous or harmful to health, which may adversely affect women's health, pregnancy or breast-feeding. The Regulation of the Council of Ministers will establish a list of work that women who are pregnant and breast-feeding will not be able to carry out. This list will include work which may have an adverse effect on the health of women, pregnancy or breast-feeding of the child due to the manner and conditions of carrying out such work or because it involves excessive physical exercise. The current 1996 regulation will still apply for a maximum period of 9 months from the date of entry into force of the Article. 176. Regulation of the Council of Ministers of 10 September 1996 on the list of work which is particularly burdensome or harmful to the health of women concerning the performance of work related to excessive physical effort, including the manual movement of burdens by non-pregnant women or during breast-feeding, will now be moved to the regulation of the Minister of Family, Labour and Social Policy of 14 March 2000 on health and safety at work in the field of transport by hand. The consequence of the amendment to the Article. 176 The Labour Code will be amended by the Act of 21 November 1967 on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland and the Act of 11 September 2003 on military service of professional soldiers. It will also be necessary to issue a new regulation on the basis of a revised Article. 48 (1) (a) 9 Act of 21 November 1967 on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! When passing on the proposal to the High House for consideration, I ask that it be dealt with in the relevant Parliamentary Committee. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u230	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present to the High House a position vis-à-vis the Government's draft Labour Code Act and certain other laws, print No 382, intended to ensure the correct transposition of the provisions of Directive 2006/54/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council on the implementation of the principle of equal opportunities and equal treatment of men and women in the field of employment, and to adapt the authorisation to the requirements of the Article. 92. 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The current wording of the Article will change as proposed. 176 The Labour Code, according to which women should not have been employed for work that is particularly burdensome or harmful to health. This provision applies to all women, whereas the Regulation adopted on the basis of it concerns mainly pregnant women and breastfeeding children. As pointed out in the explanatory memorandum, the European Commission has raised objections to this provision, recognising that it is incompatible with the provisions of the Directive on the implementation of the principle of equal treatment between men and women as regards access to employment, vocational training and promotion and working conditions. In the opinion of the European Commission, Art. 176 The Labour Code, referring to all women, and not just pregnant and breastfeeding women, is an obstacle to gender equality, particularly as regards access to employment. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the proposed changes. New wording of the article. 176 The labour code explicite, and thus clearly, clearly, will specify that pregnant women and breast-feeding women must not be employed for work that is burdensome, dangerous or harmful to health if these work can have an adverse effect on women's health, pregnancy or breast-feeding. It should be stressed that the existing wording of the Article. 176 The Labour Code did not specify that the ban on such work concerned only pregnant women and breast-feeding women. The Law and Justice Club recognises the need to cover pregnant women and women who are breastfeeding with special protection. Thus, we protect the mother and her offspring, we protect the family that is a relationship between a man and a woman, we protect the children conceived and born. In other words, we are in line with Council Directive 92/85 of October 1992 of the European Economic Community on measures to promote improvements in the safety and health at work of pregnant workers, workers who have recently given birth and breastfeeding workers. At the same time, we call on the government to ensure that the list of work that pregnant women and breastfeeding women will not be able to carry out is fully in line with the intentions set out in Council Directive 92/85 of the European Economic Community. Proposed amendments to the Article. 176 The Labour Code and some other laws will make it necessary to amend the Act on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland and the Act on the Military Service of Professional Soldiers. Both laws will include prohibitions on women from carrying out their duties, duties or duties which are dangerous, harmful or burdensome to health during pregnancy or breastfeeding. Finally, it should be stressed that the European Commission raised objections to the legislation as early as 2014. It is only regrettable that for almost two years the previous government has not attempted to amend the Labour Code and the Regulation so that they are in line with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland and the recommendations of the European Commission. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In summary, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the new draft law amending the Labour Code Act and some other laws contained in print No 382. The proposed amendment concerns Article. 176 Labour Code. Current wording of the article. 176 states that women must not be employed for work that is particularly burdensome or harmful to health. It is proposed to change by stating that pregnant and breast-feeding women will not be able to carry out work that is burdensome, dangerous or harmful to health, which may adversely affect women's health, pregnancy or breast-feeding. It is also proposed to clarify the mandate for the Council of Ministers to establish, by means of a regulation, a list of works which may have adverse effects on the health of women, the course of pregnancy or breast-feeding of a child related to excessive physical effort, including the manual transport of weights, due to the manner and conditions in which these works are carried out, taking into account the nature of the working environment and the level of their occurrence. The aim of the proposal is to align our right with the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on the implementation of the principle of equal opportunities and equal treatment between men and women in the field of employment and occupation, and to adapt the authorisation to the requirements of the Article. 92. 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The Poselski Kukiz Club15 supports the proposed amendments only because the explanatory memorandum to the bill contains a statement of maintenance in the relevant regulations of the list of work prohibited from other women, i.e. non-pregnant or non-feeder. Kukiz, 15, states that all women should be treated with respect and in a special way, because they simply deserve it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u236	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! European Union law is founded on the prohibition of discrimination. Women and men must have equal rights, especially in the labour market. Equality must concern access to employment, training, promotion or working conditions. Equality between men and women is a fundamental principle, and the Treaty on European Union also defines equality between men and women as an objective of the Community and imposes a positive obligation to support it in all its activities. The Court of Justice has always consistently recognised the right to protect the biological condition of pregnant women as well as those who are breastfeeding, and these conditions have been taken into account in the proposed amendment to the Labour Code. This does not exclude pregnant women and breastfeeding women from the labour market, but rather protects them, which the Modern will always support as a direction for development in the labour market. Moreover, the Court consistently held that the unfavourable treatment of women linked to pregnancy or maternity constitutes direct discrimination on grounds of sex. According to European regulations, each person has the right to equality and protection against discrimination. This opinion is also expressed in many judgments by the European Court of Human Rights, among others. In its judgment of 2 December 2014 concerning the prohibition of discrimination on grounds of sex, he explained that equal treatment between men and women must be objectively and reasonably justified and that promoting gender equality is currently one of the main objectives in the Member States of the Council of Europe. Therefore, these changes, this regulation are obvious and necessary. We proceed with the examination of item 14 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Tax Order and some other laws (print No 367). ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u250	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The governmental bill amending the Tax Order and some other laws, print No 367, provides for the amendment of three laws: Tax Order, Law on proceedings before administrative courts and the Act on tax on goods and services. The subject of the project is the introduction of a anti-tax avoidance clause in the Polish legal order. The project serves to seal the tax system. The anti-tax avoidance clause was already part of the Polish legal system in the past. In 2003 a tax code was introduced into the Tax Order, which was then called a tax circumvention clause. The Constitutional Tribunal held in its judgment of 2004 that a provision containing a clause was incompatible with the Constitution. Importantly, the Constitutional Tribunal then questioned only the legislative inclusion of the clause, not the very idea of combating tax avoidance by means of a clause. It is now necessary to return to this idea and implement it. The experience of Poland and other countries so far shows that the double taxation agreements themselves are not sufficient to effectively prevent tax avoidance. The standard in other economically developed countries is a general clause against tax avoidance or an developed line of case law against tax fraud. The European Commission has highlighted the risks of tax avoidance by recommending Member States to implement anti-fraud measures. Poland is one of the few countries that have not yet implemented such solutions. The Polish tax system is not immune to tax avoidance measures. On the contrary, with the progress of integration of the Polish economy into the world economy, it must be assumed that pathological behaviour already diagnosed and combated in other countries will also be more intense and severe for public finances in Poland. The consulting sector has been very active in the creation, promotion and implementation of tax avoidance schemes in recent years. Avoiding taxation is not only dangerous to public finances, but, equally important, it violates equal taxation, giving the opportunity to build unfairly market advantages over companies wishing to pay fair taxes in Poland. The introduction of a tax avoidance clause will at the same time set a limit for permissible tax optimization. The clause will have a positive impact on market competitiveness and ensure equal treatment of taxable persons engaged in economic activities. The proposed solutions take into account the recommendations of the Constitutional Court and the recommendations of the European Commission of December 2012 and benefit from the experience of other countries in combating tax avoidance. As regards the procedures for applying the clause, the project is mainly based on proven French, Australian, Canadian and British solutions. The draft assumes that the clause will apply to artificial activities carried out primarily in order to achieve a tax advantage contrary to the objective of the Tax Act. The draft proposes that the clause should apply to all taxes, with the exception of goods and services tax. Due to the specific nature of VAT for the purposes of this tax, it is proposed to introduce a definition of abuse of the right to the Act on VAT on goods and services. The proposal introduces a number of protective measures to protect taxpayers' interests from abuse of the clause. The proposal proposes that the clause be applied in the event of a tax advantage exceeding the limit of 100 000. zł. Below this limit, it will not be possible to apply the clause at all. The project provides for the sole competence of the Minister of Finance in the application of the clause, without the possibility to delegate this power to subordinate bodies. This is intended to ensure the correct and uniform application of the clause. The project involves the appointment of an expert body - the Anti-Tax Avoidance Council. The Council's task will be to assess the appropriateness of applying the clause in individual cases. The Council will be composed of representatives of various professional groups. The Council will strengthen the predictability of the application of the clause. The draft provides for the possibility of correcting the declaration in the final stage of the tax avoidance procedure. If this option is exercised, the proceedings will be terminated and the taxpayer will be protected from a dimensional decision. The proposal introduces the possibility for the taxpayer to request a protective opinion from the Minister of Finance. Safeguard opinions will be issued if the legal structure envisaged by the taxpayer is not a tax avoidance. Overcoming the six-month deadline for the opinion will result in the so-called. a silent security opinion - a presumption of a positive settlement of the taxpayer's application. Protective opinions will give a high level of legal certainty if the taxpayer is not sure about the qualification of the planned actions. The amendments proposed in the Law on proceedings before administrative courts provide for the subjecting of protective opinions and amendments to the opinions protecting judicial review. It is important that the proposed solutions enter into force as soon as possible to reduce the tax gap. The project proposes a deadline of 30 days from the date of publication of the law. In presenting the above, I propose that the submitted project be submitted for further work. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u251	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u252	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending the Tax Order and some other laws, print No 367. The main objective of the proposed amendment is to introduce a tax avoidance clause in Polish legislation, which aims to remedy the situation that we have been observing especially for several years, namely the widening of the tax gap. In fact, all available analyses of research centres analysing the state of public finances indicate that this problem in Poland is a particularly serious problem and causes significant damage to the state budget. At the same time, it should be noted that this globalisation of tax avoidance observed in all developed economies, using different types of cross-border schemes, forces a unified fight against tax avoidance. The experience of Poland so far, but also of other countries, shows that the double taxation agreements themselves are not sufficient to effectively prevent tax avoidance. Therefore, using international experience, including those just mentioned by the Minister, the experience that has proven to be successful in combating tax avoidance, the proposed bill proposes to return to the concept of a tax avoidance clause with the establishment of independent expert and social supervision. The introduction into the Polish legal order of the anti-tax avoidance clause will regulate the tax system, setting limits for permissible tax optimization, and strengthen the autonomy of tax law towards civil law. The anti-tax avoidance clause, as mentioned by the Minister, will apply when measures with the characteristics of aggressive tax optimisation result in a tax advantage exceeding 100,000. zł. Of course, it is also important that this law minimises the effects of the anti-tax avoidance clause's entry into force. The draft therefore proposes to introduce safeguards to enable taxpayers to know the position of the tax administration in relation to the planned or already undertaken transactions for which a clause may be applied. The opinions will assess whether the legal structure envisaged by the taxpayer constitutes an abuse of the freedom to shape legal relations justifying the application of the general anti-tax avoidance clause. Predictability and a balanced application of the clause in individual cases will strengthen the independent opinion panel - Anti-Tax Avoidance Board, the appointment of which is provided for by the proposed bill. Thus, the Polish anti-tax avoidance system will benefit as much as possible from the standards provided for taxpayers with a high degree of legal protection developed by other countries. The formula of security opinions and the expert panel is only available in France, Canada and Australia. The proposed normative inclusion of the anti-tax avoidance clause takes into account the guidelines of the Constitutional Court and the experience of other countries and the recommendations of the European Commission. High Izbo! Given the provisions of the Act, which on the one hand are provisions protecting taxpayers' rights and, on the other hand, those provisions proposed by the government - indeed give hope to increase the revenue of the state budget by eliminating mechanisms that today allow tax evasion... These records therefore appear to be as reasonable as possible. The impact of this law on the state budget can be said to be based on data from other countries where the clause is applied on average in 2 to 90 cases per year. It is estimated that the number of such cases in Poland will not be higher than on average about 46, that is average respect. Decisions taken in connection with the application of the substantive clause will cover significant amounts. Assuming that the amount of the reduction resulting from a single decision will amount to about PLN 2,2 million, the direct financial impact of the application of the clause related to the decisions issued by the Minister of Finance can be estimated at PLN 50 million in 2017 and PLN 100 million in 2018 and in the following years, but the indirect effects will already be on the State budget. [[♪ The bell ♪]] much more significant and will range from 1.3 billion PLN to 5.4 billion PLN per year. In view of the above, I request that the proposal be submitted to the High House for further work. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u264	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! Act K4/03. In that judgment, the Court expressly outlined the framework for future regulation, on which compliance with the tax avoidance clause in the Polish tax system was conditional. And so he considered it to be a violation of constitutional standards, and I quote: the vague and vague formulation of a provision which creates uncertainty for its addressees as to their rights and obligations by creating an overly broad framework for bodies applying such a provision, which in fact must replace the legislator with regard to issues regulated in a vague and vague manner. In addition, in the same judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal also stated that the principle of confidence in the State and the law should apply to the definition of law, in particular where tax law is concerned, allowing only the possibility of introducing the so-called law. General clauses under strict conditions. Unfortunately, despite the assurances made by the Minister, a representative of the Law and Justice Club in this revision of the Tax Order, you did not wish to take the caselaw from the Court's judgment. By submitting a draft tax avoidance clause to the High House, constitutional standards have been grossly violated. The draft amendment raises important concerns about the lack of clear definitions that allow for considerable interpretation by tax authorities, in particular the consideration of the activities carried out by the tax law's objective. The tax law does not define the purpose of the law. I know that you have looked at EU law, only in EU law in tax laws are clearly in the preamble to the defined objectives of these laws. There are no such goals in Polish legislation. Thus, this will create a free interpretation for tax authorities. The method of action was also considered to be artificial on the basis of the criterion of reasonable action, of course set arbitraryly by the tax authorities. Moreover, there are no criteria for determining what is to be understood as the materiality of the economic or economic objectives indicated by the taxpayer in the context of taking steps primarily to achieve tax benefits. This relevance will also only be assessed by the tax authorities. It is therefore necessary to fully divide the positions that are already presented in the literature, that the draft amendment is a specific attack on the rights of taxpayers, introduces sanctioned civil self-will. Also the waiting time for an opinion up to 6 months in our assessment is too long, in particular with regard to the actions planned by the taxpayer. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Finally, attention should also be paid to the short period of vacathio legis. In view of the above, as well as the elements related to the reduction of the tax gap, we will request that this law be referred to committee work. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u265	Thank you very much. Ms Genowef Tokarska, a club of the Polish People's Party, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u266	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present my position towards the government draft law amending the Tax Order and some other laws, print No 367. The draft law amends the Tax Ordination Act by introducing a new section - section IIIa First of all, I would like to point out that the new rules have been designed so that the services of the finance department apply the anti-tax avoidance clause only to large taxpayers. According to the draft, the clause will not apply to transactions for which the tax advantage will not exceed 100,000. zł. The revision of the Tax Order introduces new definitions, among others. the definition of tax avoidance, which means an action carried out to achieve a tax advantage which, in the circumstances, is contrary to the object and purpose of the Tax Act and the mode of action of the taxpayer is artificial. As examples of artificial activities, unjustified division of operations, involvement of intermediary entities or taking actions whose economic or economic risks outweigh the expected benefits has been identified. In particular, tax avoidance involves abuse of tax preferences, abuse of international agreements, the use of tax havens and the financial benefit of reducing the tax burden, or even tax reimbursement. The introduction of the anti-tax avoidance clause may raise some doubts, may be considered as an attack on the rights of the taxpayer and the freedom to shape the content of contracts. However, a number of positive opinions on the clause should be noted, resulting from assessments of the Supreme Audit Chamber, courts and interpretations of the European Parliament. We must be aware of the existence of even multinational corporations using the weaknesses of tax systems, which have at their disposal tax advisors practicing aggressive tax planning practices that avoid taxation. The negative phenomenon of tax avoidance exists in every market economy country, and the pursuit of paying as low as possible taxes is, after all, a common trend. Similar anti-tax avoidance clauses are now quite common in the world and have been in some countries for decades. The oldest clauses appeared already in the 19th century, e.g. In New Zealand and Australia, Germany introduced this standard in 1919 and later in France. The effect of the clause is the possibility for tax authorities to recover unpaid taxes. Its preventive role also needs to be stressed. Taxpayers are aware of the risks and consequences arising from the correction of the accounts, payment of the tax due together with interest, even in the absence of any other financial penalties. The draft law introducing the clause also establishes an expert and social supervisor on the application of the clause, and this is the Anti-Tax Avoidance Council appointed by the Minister of Finance. The Council's task will be to assess the application of the clause in individual cases, which will contribute to greater objectivity of decisions issued by the Authority. An important novel is also the responsibility of the Minister of Finance to issue the so-called. the protective opinion in which it will state, at the taxpayer's request, whether the activities it reports do not lead to tax avoidance. To sum up, High Izbo, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party is in favour of transferring the project to further work, because this project, although it raises some doubts, has a pro-state character, sealing the tax system, it is used to combat circumvention of tax law occurring - I will remind you once again - on a large scale, because when this benefit is above 100,000. We also hope that a realistic competitive environment for honest entrepreneurs. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u280	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I would like to present information on the 2014 National Drug Prevention Programme, which is part of the broader programme foreseen for 2011/2016. This is a continuation of the programme developed in 1999. The actions adopted in the programme are appropriate to identified drug problems, as indicated by the analysis of the data contained in this document. This material is very detailed and consists of two parts. The first part describes the phenomenon of drug addiction from an epidemiological perspective. The latest available data on the dissemination, use of drugs, health and social consequences of drug use and an overall analysis of trends observed are presented. The activities undertaken by the various actors in the implementation of the National Drug Prevention Programme are then described and an analysis of the expenditure incurred in countering drug addiction is presented. The second part of the document, or annexes, contains a table together with the values of monitoring indicators for the implementation of the National Drug Prevention Programme in 2014. Although this document is intended to present the activities undertaken by the individual bodies identified in the Regulation on the National Drug Prevention Programme for the period 2011-2016, the information in question contains an additional summary, conclusions and recommendations on further measures to combat drug addiction. In addition, given the current challenges of drug policy, as for new psychoactive substances, commonly referred to as boosters, the document was supplemented with information on this issue. When analysing the information contained in this document, there is no doubt that areas need to be improved and further stepped up, such as: the need to increase the use of addiction prevention programmes that have been recommended, to develop health harm reduction programmes, to take action on social reintegration, to increase the availability of a substitution treatment programme, to increase the role of local self-government units and to adapt the therapeutic offer to the needs of drug users. Nevertheless, I would like to draw attention to the parameters that have been improved and stabilised as a result of the strategy adopted. It should be noted that, with the exception of cannabis products, the use of drugs in the Republic of Poland remains stable. The use of new psychoactive substances by young people has been reduced three times, so-called. boosters, from 7% in 2010 to 2% in 2013, and the level of use of drugs by young people has been stabilised for drug use. On the other hand, on the basis of a study conducted at the turn of 2014 and 2015 it can be concluded that in the adult population, the proportion of users of cannabis products decreased compared to 2010, indicating that the data in this respect are stable and in the longer term it is possible to halt this phenomenon and to decrease it further. Information on the implementation of the National Drug Prevention Programme in 2014 also provides stable data on the treatment of stationary infectious diseases associated with injecting drug administration and the reduction in deaths due to drug overdose. Improvements in substitution therapy should be highlighted. Although it still doesn't cover 25% opioid addicts, there is an increase in the number of persons covered by this treatment and an increase in the amount of funds allocated to this purpose by the National Health Fund. In addition, an increase in the amount of NFZ funds for outpatient treatment should be noted, as well as an increase in the number of persons participating in programmes to reduce the risk of health and social harm to drug users. One of the key areas of anti-drug prevention is prevention, i.e. all measures aimed at preventing, delaying or reducing the use of psychoactive substances and associated negative effects. Competent government authorities, their supervised institutions and local authorities are required to carry out these tasks. In 2014 local governments of all voivodships carried out activities in the field of prevention of drug addiction. Universal prevention at all levels of school education was one of the priority actions undertaken by 14 voivodships. In addition, programmes in the field of selective prevention and prevention were supported and implemented by government administrations and institutions supervised by them and local authorities. It is also important to mention the numerous activities undertaken by the National Office for the Prevention of Drug Addiction, such as a number of publications, leaflets, brochures, whistleblowers dedicated to promoting healthy lifestyles, prevention of drug addiction, the effects of addiction and the reduction of health damage, also available online. The experience gained from the implementation of the National Drug Prevention Programme for the years 2011 to 2016 is extremely needed in the construction of a new drug strategy. Following the entry into force of the Public Health Act of 11 September 2015, the anti-drug strategy has been incorporated into the current National Health Programme 2016-2020, which is an implementing act for the abovementioned Act. The assumptions set out in this programme include holistic treatment of addictions in the context of public health threats, while identifying actions against particular types of addictions. The current solution aims at improving the cooperation of responsible institutions and integrating actions, which will translate into improving indicators that are not yet satisfactory. Thank you very much.

The National Drug Prevention Programme is difficult to develop and implement a condensed description of the state's action to regulate drug addiction through research and monitoring, prevention, treatment, rehabilitation, social reintegration, international cooperation and regulatory activities to reduce supply. The information presented on the implementation of the action resulting from the National Drug Prevention Programme creates the impression that the state institutions remain one step behind the progressive phenomenon and problem. However, such a situation seems inevitable. We create new laws, bans, algorithms of conduct related to controlling the market for psychoactive substances, which is undoubtedly needed, but will always be secondary to the already existing problem. We do not criticize the very idea of penalising acts related to banned substances, which are largely necessary and necessary. We note that the accent and the force of influence are directed towards the wrong actions. The area of addiction, regardless of the nature of substances - alcohol, drugs boosters or addictive behaviour - is not an unknown space, an unsolvable problem for which we spend millions of zlotys a year. It is a widely studied field of knowledge that knows the answers to key questions. Yes, addictions can be effectively prevented and treated effectively. However, if we engage in actions focused on creating further rules, prohibitions and sanctions, our action will not be effective, because it is not aimed at the causes of the phenomenon, but at its effects. The reason for addiction is not the availability of a substance, but the reason for which a person reaches for it. By analysing scientific publications and available research results, also through the document on which this statement relates, it is easy to believe that we have more and more tools and knowledge needed to prevent and compensate for social deficits such as addiction. With all responsibility, I can admit that this potential is too low and incorrectly exploited. This point of view is entirely in line with the conclusions presented in the reference information on the implementation of the actions resulting from the National Drug Prevention Programme. The implementation of recommended prevention programmes with proven effectiveness in Polish schools is not a standard. I would like to add that the professional preventive impact of learning new skills, particularly interpersonal, intra-psyche skills, allows us to understand ourselves, live in a way that is satisfying, solve problems and difficulties constructively without using chemicals or escaping to negative youth groups. This activity is not only the best protection against the involvement of young people in experimenting and using psychoactive measures, but it is also a guarantee of healthy development in the psychological aspect. Teachers and educators can be the best providers of prevention and action focused on prevention of addiction, not by conducting educational and informational lessons, what drugs are, only by staying in a proper relationship with their pupils, helping and supporting, remaining authority. The studies that we are referring to show that teachers are often inadequately prepared to play the role of educators, and even more so to prevent. We are convinced that the basic recommendation for information on the implementation of the National Drug Prevention Programme should be to discuss immediately the quality of teacher education in the field of psychological skills for this role and the school as a primary educational institution. There is no doubt that investment in prevention of addiction will prove to be by far the most effective activity. The most important level for human-directed interactions is the local level, the local level, where real work is carried out with people at risk of addiction or addiction. Being honest with each other and with citizens, it should be acknowledged that regulations have been created that have placed a huge burden on local governments, which play a crucial role in preventing addiction. However, self-governments do not carry out their duties sufficiently. They do not properly manage the potential of tools and skills of practitioners in prevention and addiction therapy. They are not sufficiently involved in harm reduction programmes. To a minimum, they support aftercare activities to support patients after the end of addiction treatment. These reservations must be confronted with the knowledge of the financial capacity of local governments. Even small municipalities have several hundred thousand, and sometimes even more than a few million annually, funds from the fees for the marketing of alcoholic beverages, only a small proportion of which are spent on statutory purposes, namely prevention and resolution of addiction problems, which remain beyond the control of the authorities. With regard to the issue of fundamental irregularities in the spending of municipal funds, attention should be paid to the negative ideas of coherence and cooperation in organisational arrangements at national level. The area of prevention and psychotherapy of addictions are common, regardless of the nature of the substance. Today we are talking about the National Drug Prevention Programme in complete isolation from the National Alcohol Prevention Programme. Similarly, there are key institutions in Poland that deal with addictions: the National Office for Drugs Prevention and the National Agency for Alcohol Problem Solutions. Preventive activities, organisation of training and the process of certification of therapists, research and monitoring are only some of the activities that are common to institutions that function separately. We cannot talk about cooperation with local governments if there is no cooperation at organisational level. These conclusions are very worrying. Therefore, after analysing information on the implementation of the actions resulting from the National Drug Prevention Programme, we find that the existing regulations of the widely recognised prevention and treatment of addictions in Poland are not effective and require immediate intervention. Poland has a number of institutions whose activities are focused on addictions: the National Office for Drugs Prevention, whose activities are reflected in the document referred to, the National Agency for Alcohol Problems Solutions, voivodship drug therapy centers, whose activities should be combined and not shared. We need to re-plan an efficient institutional system dealing with drug prevention, corresponding to current demand, cheaper maintenance and more efficient. Only full cooperation between addiction institutions, including research centres and the Ministry of Education, will contribute to the recommendations contained in the document presented and to a significant change in the implementation of drug prevention programmes in the coming years. Thank you. [[Applause]]

One of the most significant and effective areas of assistance for addicts is postrehabilitation, while in Poland only three NFZ branches finance hostels: Łódź, Lower Silesia and Świętokrzyski. The question is why. According to the report, the problem of drug addiction is a marginalized topic by local governments, and 57 local government units have not developed a municipal programme against addiction or drug addiction at all. Other unsolved problems remain, such as: driving under the influence of drugs such as alcohol or drugs - this remains a serious problem in the Republic of Poland. Although opiate drug use is decreasing, drug use other than opiates of substances, in particular amphetamines, mephedrones and other boosters, and non-prescription drugs are spreading. This phenomenon poses a serious threat to public health and requires continued efforts to increase the availability of needle and syringe exchange programmes and the offer of low-level measures. Finally, in addiction therapy, as in psychiatry, there is a need for a number of actors to work together to ensure that people return after therapy, cooperation between the NFZ and urban social services centres and work offices. Perhaps it would be necessary to set up a team at the Ministry of Health who, in cooperation with the Police, would undertake this task. On the other hand, the list of narcotic drugs in Annex 1 to the Anti-drugs Act itself also needs to be updated more efficiently. A change of approach also appears necessary as regards the criminal consequences of the marketing of replacement measures. Actually, it's an art. 52a of the Act provides that who manufactures or markets a substitute or a new psychoactive substance on the territory of the Republic of Poland shall be subject to a penalty of 20 thousand. up to PLN 1 million imposed in administrative mode by the State Health Inspector. Practice shows that it is almost impossible to enforce this punishment. It would therefore be appropriate to propose that the practice of law enforcement be harmonised either by amending the Article. 52a Act on the prevention of drug addiction and the change in the nature of the administrative penalty referred to therein to be typically criminal, or by removing it from that law with the simultaneous amendment of the aforementioned article. 165 Penal Code. In turn, we consider it pointless to punish the users of substitution measures with prison or with a high fine, who are primarily in need of the help of specialists. In order to reduce red tape, we believe that this amendment should be introduced together with an amendment on the authorisation of medicines from medical marijuana and medicinal cannabis varieties. This would have an impact on reducing the costs of tackling the drug problem in view of the exclusion of medical users from this fight, [[♪ The bell ♪]] which are currently forced to use the black market. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u316	Mr. Marshal! The bill was not considered in the previous term of office of the Sejm, but due to its civic character it was not subject to the principle of discounting and was re-reading today. In parallel to the civil project since July 2014, a parliamentary draft law on the profession of physiotherapist was launched, originally significantly different from the assumptions of the civil project and thus from the expectations of patients and the physiotherapists' environment. However, in the course of the multi-monthly work of the Parliament's committees and sub-committees of health and extensive public and environmental consultations, it was possible to merge both projects and to draw up such provisions of the law, which are entirely in line with the proposed provisions of the citizens' project. You bet on, among other things. direct contact with a physiotherapist and the creation of a physiotherapist self-government, which is the only guarantee of system verification and proper pursuit of a profession affecting patient safety, which is the main message of regulation. The creation of a self-government will also allow for the supervision of physiotherapists and postgraduate physiotherapists, the creation of standards and prevention programmes, which will also directly translate into billions of savings for the state budget. Today, only because of the incapacity for work of citizens concerning, according to estimates, about 6 million people in the production group, the state budget bears the cost of 30 billion PLN per year. The introduction without unnecessary time of waiting for modern physiotherapy and prevention is able to effectively minimize the risk of falling out of the labour market for health reasons and thus to reduce the risk of falling out of the labour market. reduce costs effectively. According to ZUS data for 2015, only because of the so-called. back pain Poles left over 30 million days at work. The parliamentary project, which takes into account all the demands of the citizens' project, was held and on 25 September 2015 the VII-term Sejm passed the physiotherapist's profession law, and the President of the Republic of Poland, Mr Andrzej Duda, signed the physiotherapist's profession law on 2 October. Finally, on 30 November 2015, the physiotherapist's profession was announced in the Monitor... in the Official Journal of the Laws, item. 1994, and enters into force after 6 months, i.e. May 31st this year Everyone, in particular Members directly involved in the work on the draft and the adoption of the law, as well as the Ministry and Professor Marian Zębali present, who was then Minister of Health, should be thanked at this point. [[Applause]] And, of course, President Andrzej Duda for creating such a good and nearly 30 years of expected law, the right to safe and professional physical therapy services for millions of Poles and the right to dignity and professional independence of a 70-thousand-year group of physiotherapists. The citizens' draft law on the profession of physiotherapist - we read further - in principle it contains matter, which has already been regulated in the Act of 25 September 2015. Therefore, the government does not see the justification for further proceding the citizens' bill. On the day of the publication of the government's position, we fully agreed with it and we would have agreed if there had not been any facts - there would have been as many as two proposals for amending the law, which has not yet entered into force. Manifestation has echoed widely in public and social media and has led to many fruitful meetings with representatives of this House. It completely addicts the physiotherapist to the rehabilitation doctor's orders, which in our assessment will drastically reduce the quality of services and increase the waiting time for therapy. Today, in many resorts, you wait up to 9 months. Post-brain therapy - waiting time: 9 months. It's gonna take a lot longer than that. There is no guarantee of safety for patients, but the cost of the state budget and citizens who will have to bear the additional costs of physiotherapy orders, also outside the NFZ refund system, which currently carries out more than 80% of all physiotherapeutic services. The physiotherapists' local government, which was the core of a civil project, is to keep the National Register of Physiotherapists and verify the granting of the right to pursue the profession which gives our patients the only real guarantee of safety that the person who plans and conducts their therapy has the appropriate competence. It is also about creating standards in physiotherapy and prevention that are not there today. There are no standards in our profession. Self-government is also taking care of the development and image of physiotherapy, taking care of pre- and post-graduate education and many other tasks, which according to the Ministry of Health will now meet the register kept by voivodships. ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u318	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the club Law and Justice on the citizens' draft law on the profession of physiotherapist, print number 29. The purpose of the citizens' bill is to regulate the rules for the pursuit of the profession of physiotherapist, including pre- and post-graduate training, the submission of a state examination of physiotherapy and a state specialization exam, the acquisition of the right to pursue the profession, the professional competence of the therapist and the principles of professional responsibility and the organisation of professional self-government. In principle, the initiative to regulate the profession of physiotherapist, in particular in the context of ensuring the health safety of patients, is appropriate. But I would like to point out that this citizens' draft law on the profession of physiotherapist entered the Sejm of the Republic of Poland on 30 June 2015 during the 7th term of the Sejm, despite the fact that advanced work on the parliamentary draft law on the profession of physiotherapist and on the amendment of other laws, which was submitted in the Sejm of the Republic of Poland on 23 July 2014 by Members of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, concluded the adoption of this law by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland on 25 September 2015, and as my previous speaker mentioned, on 26 October 2015, the President of the Republic of Poland signed the Act, which was announced on 30 November 2015. This initiative therefore raises legitimate concerns and concerns as to the substance of the measures already adopted, which differ from those proposed in the citizens' project. For example, the above-mentioned draft law on the profession of physiotherapist proposes a different wording of the professional tasks of physiotherapist. It is important to stress the concerns raised by the catalogue of professional tasks, in particular in the context of the various qualifications of physiotherapists. It is therefore necessary to consider the need to define professional tasks according to the level of education already obtained at the level of the law. The regulations contained in the draft law aimed at determining the requirements to be met by the applicant for the right to pursue the profession of physiotherapist, including the qualifications necessary for the pursuit of the profession in question, differ from those contained in the Law of 25 September 2015 on the profession of physiotherapist. The bill requires physiotherapists to constantly update their professional knowledge and skills in the form of specialization, training and self-education. The project sets out requirements for specialization training aimed at obtaining specialist knowledge and skills by a physiotherapist in a specific field of physiotherapy or an area applicable to health care and the title of specialist in this field. However, the provisions should be clarified. For example, the chapter should specify in detail the rules for accreditation for the conduct of specialisation training, the rules for carrying out the checks of the entities carrying out this training, the rules on how the physiotherapist is to be admitted to the qualification procedure for specialisation training, the conduct of this procedure, the holding of training in this field and the manner in which the specialisation examination is carried out. The draft law also amends the provisions in force and contains transitional and final provisions. These provisions also need to be supplemented and clarified in such a way as to allow inter alia. for the continuation of specialist training of physiotherapists during the transition period. The citizens' draft law on the profession of physiotherapist contains in principle matter, which has already been regulated in the Act of 25 September 2015 on the profession of physiotherapist. Therefore, the validity of its further processing may be questionable. However, since an interested physiotherapist community sees in the law of substantive doubt adopted on 25 September 2015 and Law and Justice does not throw civil projects into the basket, as it did to the previous power, the coalition of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party, [[♪ The bell ♪]] We are in favour of further proceding the citizens' bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u357	Mr President, I am sorry, but we are at this point at the moment when the Minister speaks. You're welcome. Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Health Marek Tombarkiewicz: I understand that Minister Zembala's statement was polemic, and I will not comment on it. When it comes to the difference between a functional diagnosis and a medical diagnosis, a medical examination is a much broader concept. The study consists of a physical examination, a physical examination, an examination of all systems, while a functional examination refers only, in this particular case, to a single organ, i.e. a locomotor organ. So, of course, there is a significant difference between the record of a physical examination that is designed to be performed by a physiotherapist and a medical examination. So that here we absolutely see a clear difference. Yes, the bill was proceded and obtained a significant consensus, but we still need to remember that there were protests of the medical community. Of course, the environment, like all, is not uniform and some doctors will certainly support the rapid entry into force of this law, such as those professional groups that are most involved in physiotherapy, namely orthopedics, neurologists, cardiochirurgeons. The next question was addressed by Mr Tomasz Kostusia. What is the difference between physiotherapeutic examination, functional examination, what is it, what are the differences with regard to medical examination? This accusation has been made to us throughout the legislative process: that we want to enter - I apologize for the expression - into the doctor's shoes that we will now replace doctors, we will examine patients. Ladies and gentlemen, in the physiotherapist's law of 25 September 2015 in Article. 2 is written that the physiotherapist performs a functional examination, not a medical examination. What's the difference between a functional examination and a medical examination? In 2001, the World Health Organization defined - this is not our invention - the World Health Organization - a model of functional research, defining them as ICF, International Classification of Functioning, Disability and Health, indicating unambiguously that the ICF concept study, functional testing, belongs to the same group of tests, which includes the ICD-10 medical diagnosis. They must exist at the same time, not replace each other. Medical examination gives us a diagnosis, gives us a diagnosis. A proverbial stroke is a medical diagnosis. We physiotherapists don't diagnose strokes like we don't diagnose cancers, just like we don't diagnose a number of other diseases or injuries. On the other hand, a functional examination determines how a disease or injury reduces in everyday life, which is not present in a medical diagnosis. A functional study is supposed to show what limitations exist, what it means, whether a patient after a stroke can climb down stairs, can get out of bed, go to the toilet, and doesn't need help there, or is capable of taking a job. It's a functional study, it's the subject of our work, the physical therapist's work. What does that mean? This means that not every person who has the same diagnosis has the same limitations at the level of functioning in everyday life and vice versa. [[That's right.]] People who have a similar level of operating limitations can easily be imagined. A man can't go down the stairs after a stroke, but a man with an endoprothesis can't go down the stairs or a man with a back pain can also have difficulty climbing the stairs sometimes. This does not mean that people who have the same level of functioning have the same disease, and only a common medical examination, medical examination, giving diagnosis and diagnosis and defining all kinds of contraindications and underlying diseases, and functional examination, which are performed by physiotherapists, gives a complete picture of the patient's health status. The physiotherapeutic examination we perform gives the basis for planning the improvement process, because there is a defined problem. Today, the National Health Fund does not carry out functional diagnostics. What does that mean? This means that physiotherapy is planned only on the basis of a list of treatments defined by the NFZ. Patients in Poland have been undergoing treatments for 50 years. Physiotherapy is not going to the movies, ladies and gentlemen, physiotherapy is a treatment. We're also involved in the treatment process, we're treating with motion. We need to define the problem, define it and solve it, which is to select the tools. Our work, our work tools are selected according to the defined problem, cannot be defined before. This is a mistake. in art, for 15 years in the world this is considered an error in art. Today it is the standard in the National Health Fund. Let's talk about it openly, these are mistakes in the physical therapist's art. Why? Because we were completely shut down, we were beyond the margin of influence on our work. After 5 years of study, a physical therapist receives a referral and an order to do what he has to do. Then why do we need college? Why do we need college? To study for five years to do a 15-minute suspension order? An average 15-year-old will do this. You don't have to educate physiotherapists in college for five years. And our education doesn't end in college, we're postgraduate, each of us spends a lot of thousands of hours in postgraduate courses. We've been training our whole lives and we just want to do our job properly, that's all. I hope that this is an exhaustive answer to the question of how the functional examination differs from the medical examination and why it is necessary to perform it at the same time.
