ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u360	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the European Union Commission, I have the honour to present to the High House the reasons for adopting a resolution on the recognition of a draft directive of the European Parliament and of the Council amending Directive 96/71/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 December 1996 concerning the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services as incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The European Commission has presented a proposal to amend Directive 96/71/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council, which is premature and undermines the joint obligations of the institutions of the European Union and the Member States to adopt legislation based on prior evaluation of existing solutions in a given area, which should reduce the regulatory burden imposed. In the Committee's view, Member States' efforts should focus on the correct implementation of the implementation directive adopted in 2014. This Directive offers Member States appropriate and sufficient instruments to combat abuse and unfair competition, as well as to protect posted workers. In examining the changes proposed by the European Commission, the European Union's Affairs Commission has highlighted a number of important issues. Firstly, the free movement of workers, freedom of establishment and the freedom to provide services are fundamental principles of the internal market in the European Union contained in the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. The changes introduced by the European Commission infringe the freedom of the internal market and consequently, according to the Commission for European Union Affairs, the principle of subsidiarity, which is apparent by changing the wording of the Article. 3 (1) (a) 1 Directive. Secondly, the proposal lacks any justification for the compatibility of the proposed directive with the principle of subsidiarity. Such justification cannot be regarded as a single sentence stating that, I quote, the existing Directive can only be amended by adopting a new directive. Thirdly, the absence of such justification and, in particular, the absence of any qualitative and quantitative indicators constitutes an infringement of Article. Five minutes to protocol two. Moreover, it makes it significantly more difficult if it does not prevent national Parliaments from exercising their power to control respect for the principle of subsidiarity. Here the legal basis is included in the article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3, Article 12 lit. b the Treaty on European Union and Article. 6 of Protocol No 2. And fourthly, also designed art. 3 (1) (a) 1b Directive 96/71 of the European Communities, which is to replace the existing Article. 3 (1) (a) 9 directives violate the principle of subsidiarity. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission for European Union Affairs also took note of the views of the social side and the opinions of employers' organisations. According to the committee, each party has shown great responsibility for the environment it represents. Among other things, NSZZ.Solidarity pointed out that the competitiveness of Polish companies cannot be built on the basis of lower labour costs, as relying solely on such comparative advantages will in the long term reduce the quality of human capital and will not help the Polish economy move up the value added chain. On the other hand, employers' organisations have pointed out that the restrictions that may be encountered in the implementation of the changes proposed by the European Commission will increase unemployment, especially in the construction sector, in not only the border area but also in the whole country, thereby reducing PIT and CIT tax revenues. The hearing of the committee on the social side confirms that the social dialogue on changes related to the evaluation of the draft Directive 96/71 of the European Communities has been carried out and has given specific impetus to the work of the committee. On behalf of the committee, I thank the social side for its active participation in the work of the European Union Committee on Affairs. Unfortunately, the same cannot be said about dialogue at European Union level. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u362	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the contested resolution. High Izbo! Posting workers plays an important role in cross-border provision of services. The possibility of providing services in other Member States, in particular for the small and medium-sized enterprises sector, represents an opportunity to develop activities throughout Europe. Posting of workers creates business opportunities and opportunities for employment, creates additional income in sending countries and contributes to improving competitiveness and efficiency in host countries. These are mutual benefits. On the other hand, the amendments to the proposed directive will hit entrepreneurs from the countries of Central and Eastern Europe in the first place. In this context, the most serious concerns are the introduction of a posting solution lasting more than 24 months or the introduction of a wage requirement covering all components of the remuneration in force in the country concerned. It should be noted that the observed pay gap between the Member States of the European Union is the result of the remaining differences at the level of economic development and cannot be seen as unfair competition. The introduction of the proposed changes would imply additional costs for the entrepreneur from the sending countries, which could result in a reduction in the number of posted workers from Poland. Here it is estimated that in the European Union nearly 2 million workers are posted annually by their employers to work in another Member State. Poland is the country from which the most workers are posted, and therefore the most important consequences are the reduction in the activity of small and medium-sized enterprises. Increased costs on the part of Polish employers will reduce the competitiveness of Polish companies and reduce resources for business development. Further, increasing barriers to the free activity of companies from other countries within the European Union will adversely affect economic growth in the European Union due to a decrease in competition and an increase in service prices for end-users. Many other negative consequences could be mentioned here. Such initiatives should be considered discriminatory as they undermine the interests of a group of Member States, as well as disproportionate ones, as the stated regulatory objectives can be otherwise achieved by using the existing legal tools provided for inter alia. in the framework of the European Union Platform for the Prevention of Unregistered Work or, more broadly, in the 2014 Implementation Directive mentioned by the rapporteur. This proposal violates the subsidiarity principle because the proposed directive does not ensure that the objective of the intended action at European Union level is achieved in a better way than through action at national level. Therefore, the Law and Justice will vote in favour of the draft resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u368	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It should be stated that the resolution of the Sejm on the recognition of a draft directive of the European Parliament and of the Council amending Directive 96/71/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 December 1996 concerning the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services is a valid resolution. The objectives of the draft directive are incompatible with the subsidiarity principle, which is one of the fundamental principles of the European Union. The project itself does not contain any justification for its compliance with the above principle. The amendments proposed in this Directive may raise questions of interpretation and design, because they are incomprehensible and incompatible with the correct legislative technique. They clearly lead to the restriction of freedom to provide services and the emergence of legal gaps. In addition, they are incompatible with European law and substantially restrict the competitiveness of posting companies. This directive aims at seemingly countering unfair practices and ensuring respect for workers' rights. The changes envisaged in the project will actually lead to a reduction in the scale of posting of workers, which will have a number of negative consequences on the labour markets. First of all, there will be a reduction in the competitiveness of Polish entrepreneurs and even their exclusion from European markets, which will naturally result in an increase in unemployment. There is particular doubt about the introduction of a regulation that, in the case of a posting of more than 24 months to a posted worker, the labour law of the host State should apply from the first day of posting, when it was known in advance that it would exceed 24 months and from the first day following the end of 24 months where it could not be foreseen that the posting would exceed 24 months. It is not justified to adopt such a position because none of the regulations of the European Union give rise to the adoption of a period of 24 months as a determinant to the conclusion that we are dealing with a country where work is normally provided. Delegating in the light of such regulations will no longer be simply cost-effective. The proposed directive also provides for a departure from the wording of the minimum wage rate for the purpose of payment. The new term is not specified and will be subject to any interpretation, given that under that term all components of remuneration required by national law, regulations or administrative provisions, collective agreements or arbitration decisions applicable under the Directive will have to be understood. This will result in a reduction in the cross-border provision of services using their own employees. The social partners representing employers who took part in the Commission's deliberations on European Union affairs also commented very negatively on this directive, stressing that the proposed proposal would increase the uncertainty of cross-border activities by introducing complex legal regulations. In addition, they pointed out, [[♪ The bell ♪]] that it raises doubts in the light of the Treaty principle of freedom to provide services. Modern will support the draft resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u369	Thank you. And please take the floor of Mr Paul Beda.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u370	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council amending the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 December 1996 concerning the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services does not comply with the subsidiarity principle referred to in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 of the Treaty on European Union. This proposal violates the subsidiarity principle because the proposed Directive does not ensure that the objectives of the intended action at Union level are achieved in a better way than those of national action. The stated objective of the draft directive is to address unfair practices and promote the principle that the same work at the same place should be remunerated. These objectives are to be achieved by requiring the worker to be covered by the labour law of the country to which he is posted, where the period of posting is longer than 24 months, by changing the remuneration of such workers from the minimum wage rate together with the overtime rate for remuneration together with the overtime rate and by establishing an equal remuneration for posted workers in the sub-contractors' chains and for the main contractor's employees by applying the working conditions applied by the main contractor. The application of the minimum wage currently in force in European Union law in a Member State of the European Union, within whose territory the worker is posted, allows for adequate social protection of workers, while at the same time taking into account the natural differences in the level of economic development between Member States which translate into differences in the level of remuneration. Further equalisation of the level of wages in the Member States should be the result of the gradual economic development of the Member States and not of the European Union's legislative action. The fact that the Directive can be amended by the adoption of a new directive does not mean that the solutions proposed as amendments to the existing Directive must be introduced at European Union level at all. The Commission has in no way justified that the objectives it proposes to amend the Directive cannot be better regulated at national level. The absence of such justification, in particular the absence of any qualitative and quantitative indicators, constitutes an infringement of Article. The 5 minutes, moreover, make it significantly more difficult if they do not make it impossible for national Parliaments to exercise their power to check respect for the principle of subsidiarity. In the current legal state, Member States may provide that undertakings posting temporary workers must guarantee them the conditions applicable to temporary workers in the Member State in which the work is carried out. According to the impact assessment, 15 Member States have benefited from this possibility. Others, including Poland, have not introduced any regulations in this respect. The existing European Union law allows Member States to align the rights of temporary workers posted to work in another Member State with those of temporary workers employed by temporary employment agencies established in the Member State in which the work is carried out. [[♪ The bell ♪]] There is no obstacle for Member States to adopt such legislation on the basis of an analysis of the labour market with regard to safeguarding the interests of businesses and workers. Where Member States consider that such provisions are necessary in order to ensure a level playing field for undertakings operating on their territory, they may introduce them. According to the data provided by the European Commission, more than half of the Member States have introduced such legislation. It should be considered that those countries which did not take such measures considered that they were not necessary to safeguard the interests of businesses and workers. It should be considered that Member States can better assess with effect than the European Commission whether the rights of posted temporary workers need to be aligned. There is no need for this issue to be regulated uniformly in all countries of the European Union. The Commission has not demonstrated that the Member States alone cannot sufficiently achieve the objective set out by it. Polish People's Party will support the proposed bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u438	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Modern Club, I present our position on the amendments of the Senate to the Act amending the Act on Explosives for Civil Use and some other laws. On 18 March 2016, the Sejm adopted a law amending the Act on Explosives for Civil Use. The aim of the amendment was to implement the provisions of the three European Union directives from 2013 to 2014 in order to increase safety in the design, manufacture, use and transport of explosives. The Senate has introduced 11 amendments to the Act in order to organize, standardize, supplement the provisions of the Act. The Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, after considering the Senate resolution at its meeting on 13 April 2016, proposed that the Sejm adopt amendments from 1 to 9 and 11 and reject amendment 10. On behalf of the Modern Club, I agree with the position of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, because the Senate amendments do not really interfere with the merits of the case, but improve the passed law in terms of legislative level. In part, the Senate's position on Amendment 10 can also be divided, which deletes the provisions imposing an obligation on an explosive distributor to act with due care with regard to the requirements of the Act. The Senate proposed to delete this provision not to exempt the distributor from the requirement to act with due care, but because such a requirement was not laid down in the law by the manufacturer or importer, which might suggest that the exemption from due diligence... Since that was not the purpose of the bill, the Senate considers that the contested provision concerning the distributor is unnecessary. Logically, the Senate is right, but Directive 2014/28/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 February 2014 contains such an obligation relating specifically to the distributor, which justifies leaving it, as it were, to the bill and rejecting the Senate amendment. Finally, on behalf of the Modern Club, I ask the High Sejm to adopt the committee's position on the Senate amendments, i.e. to accept amendments 1 to 9 and 11 and reject Amendment 10. Thank you. These State Forests were formed from the forests that belonged to the property of the three conquerors, and then on these foundations three principles were created, in fact, by the President's decrees. The first is that the State Forests are to be accessible to the population in terms of use, let's call it recreational, collection of forest fruits, etc. The second thing is that they are supposed to use the general plan, i.e. to produce good, but at the same time the plan assumed that the goods to be consumed should be more at the end of the plan than at the beginning, i.e. the typical approach which is now called sustainable development in the European Union. And third, they're supposed to be self-financing. As for the third rule, I must say it was not followed. Why is that? I would like to inform you of the phenomenon of sponsored protests regarding the amendment of the law on the construction of wind farms in our country. Today, the Law and Justice Government is carrying out a widely understood action of good change, which is shown by good reforms on many levels of social, political and economic life. One of the areas covered by the good change is the protection of the environment. In this respect, the government introduces a distance regime to regulate wind farms at an appropriate distance from human habitats and other areas to which wind farms have a negative impact. Although this law aims to protect people and the environment from the side-effects of energy generation from large wind farms, many protests have voiced that this is an alleged attack on clean energy. Protests are being announced to defend the existing regulations, indicating the negative attitude of the government in environmental matters. I've decided to look into these protests. It turned out that the announced protests are not only organized, but also sponsored by large wind farms. Free tours with a tour of Warsaw are organized, in which not only transport is provided, but also catering, but also taking part in a rally against the new government regulations. Such protests, instead of spontaneous expression of the will of society, are sponsored by international large corporations by gathering unknowing or misleading random people. To confirm my words, I present a leaflet informing you about a trip to Warsaw, which is organized by one of the Polish travel agencies and whose sponsor is the company Green Bear Corporation Poland - a corporation that deals with setting up wind farms. The tour is presented as an ordinary, completely free meal, including a free tourist trip, in which participation in the rally against government plans for wind farms is included. It is also interesting to know where to register for the trip. In addition to the travel agency, the School-Preschool Szadowice team is involved in this, in the secretariat of which it is possible to sign up for this trip and thus politically. In this way, large corporations involve an apolitical institution, such as a school dedicated to raising and teaching children and young people, in political action. The extent of social support that has been obtained in recent elections in order to make a good change in Poland, including in the field of environmental protection, should therefore continue. Knowing whether protests are a form of political sponsorship against a good change introduced by our government rather than a true expression of the will of society. The public expressed its will at ballot boxes in October 2015. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u497	Thank you very much. Mrs Iwona Michalek, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u498	Statement on the maintenance of the College Medicum of Nicolaus Copernicus University within the current structure of the UMK. In autumn 2015, several politicians of the Civic Platform from Bydgoszcz invented a way for their election campaign to parliament: dividing the best university in northern Poland, i.e. the Nicolaus Copernicus University in Toruń. They thought it necessary to fuel and intensify the Toruń-Bedgo animosities. They want to destroy the best brand in the Kujawsko-Pomorskie Voivodeship. The project written for the campaign not only has no substantive justification but is also legally defective. It is no wonder that it has been negatively assessed by all institutions affecting the development of higher education in Poland, such as the Conference of Rectors of Academic Schools of Poland, the Conference of Rectors of Polish Universities and the Main Council of Science and Higher Education. In the last position we read, quote: The project does not contain a substantive justification of purpose, and especially the necessity to separate the Collegium Medicum. Nicolaus Copernicus University in Toruń and the Medical Academy in Bydgoszcz in 2003 made an autonomous and voluntary decision to join. This was because both environments saw an opportunity to build a strong scientific environment. It was already realized that the future of Polish science was cooperation and integration, not division. History was right to the authors of this idea. Today, both communities form the fifth university in the country and the strongest public university in northern Poland. The medical development of the University and the entire university is impressive. The number of directions in Collegium Medicum has increased from seven to 22. The number of doctoral and habilitation rights has doubled. The financial situation of the Collegium Medicum, whose revenues increased significantly from 44 million in 2003 to 141 million last year, improved significantly. The total value of assets increased at that time from PLN 152 million to PLN 416 million. The College Medicum enjoys full organisational and financial autonomy. Every part of the university benefits from cooperation. The draft law is contrary not only to national, but also to European higher education and science policies. It is not by chance that the current Higher Education Act does not include the possibility of dividing universities. On the contrary, the strength of Polish science requires consolidation and cooperation of universities, which we have more in Poland today than in Germany or the UK. The most bizarre fact of this project is that at no stage of its preparation, the academic community concerned was not consulted, so that the project itself assumes the division of Nicolaus Copernicus University without the agreement and opinion of the academic senate. This is a violation of the principle of autonomy of universities enshrined in the Constitution, as well as of the provisions of the Law on Higher Education, which leaves no doubt that such an opinion is required for all changes in the organisation of universities. Meanwhile, the university authorities and its community oppose the separation of the Collegium Medicum. This is demonstrated by numerous statements by the Rector's authorities, as well as the recently adopted resolution of the University Senate on the preservation of the integrity of the university. The bill is legally defective not only in the context of the most important principles contained in the Constitution and the Law on Higher Education - it also violates a number of other rules and principles. The law in force does not allow for the autonomous transfer of workers from one unit to another. Unsolved remains the question of students who started their studies, hoping to obtain a UMK diploma, one of the best universities in Poland, and not the Medical University in Bydgoszcz, which will only build its position. There can be no agreement to destroy the best brand in Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship, which is Nicolaus Copernicus University. There's no permission to do that. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u506	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In the 1950s, the authorities of the Polish People's Republic began to create military formations with a particular economic purpose, which, due to their functions, were not fully military. Among them are mining, railway, road or construction troops. In fact, those who served under the cover of military service were forced and used for hard work, always under very difficult conditions. It was common practice to include in such battalions and regiments persons suspected or considered by communist authorities as enemies of the system, e.g. because of family background or service in the Home Army. Therefore, the elements of repression in the form of coercion of torture work were applied to them, while simultaneously derogating the living, sanitary and nutritional conditions. A step towards making amends for forced labor was the adoption of the Law of 2 September 1994 on the provision of money and powers to soldiers of the military substitute service forced to employ in coal mines, quarryings, uranium ore plants and construction battalions. According to its records, the former soldiers of these formations serving in 1949 and 1959 have the right to provide money and a lump sum of energy for each full month of service, but not more than 20 months from now. Unfortunately, in the above law, people serving in the railway forces who operated in our country until 1989 were omitted. They were most often the inhabitants of the eastern part of the country, about which the communist authorities had suspicions of a hostile attitude to the system at that time. Hence, the appointments to the railway forces and the forced work in the construction of tracks were one of the forms of repression used against the insubordinate Poles. As a result of the great exercise and the lack of regular meals, work lasted 12 hours a day regardless of the weather conditions in most workers caused negative health effects. There have also been fatal accidents on construction sites. According to estimates, these soldiers built or modernised about 40% Polish railway lines. For their efforts, they received only modest wages. Today they live in a sense of harm and have been fighting unsuccessfully for many years for their hard service. I therefore ask Minister Elżbieta Rafalska to undertake work to establish a witness for former rail troops, which will be an expression of social justice and a form of historical memory for them. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u507	Thank you. Mr Krzysztof Głuchowski, Law and Justice have the floor.

The railway junction of Łuków supports connections to Dęblin, Siedlec, Warsaw and towards Terespol. Used to be ticket companies sold tickets for all calls. There isn't even one at the moment. This is the case from 30 September 2014. I would like to mention that, in addition to ticket sales, the cashiers were happy to provide information about train running throughout Poland. Currently, passengers from the district of Łukow using the station of PKP are deprived of such and other information, and with constant changes in train timetables it is a huge difficulty for them. Not everyone can operate a ticket machine, not everyone has access to the Internet. Most travelers try to buy a ticket on a train, but it often happens that they have to stand in line for a ticket on a speeding train. The owner of the PKP SA station does not see this problem, and the carriers using the Łuków station in their letters explain that maintaining the ticket offices requires economic justification. Each side is on its own. And where is the social aspect of this matter? Where is the passenger's interest, its comfort of travel and the standards in the civilized world? Currently the works related to the modernisation of the PKP station by the Polish State Railways SA are being carried out. This entity, although investing, does not affect the provision of services to passengers at the PKP Łuków station. The work is expected to be completed at the end of June 2016. After the work is done, the comfort of travel is to be improved. How if cash registers and other service points are still not open? Someone's gonna say the money's too expensive. Why? Because the opening of the cash register is not interested in carriers. The reason for this is probably high prices for the provision of space by PKP SA. Their maintenance is of course a cost, but the higher standard and comfort of passenger travel, its habits and habits should also be taken into account, especially if they are young people commuting to upper secondary schools, universities and the first job. The costs could be lower if several carriers were sold in the same cashiers. Unfortunately, the common system was terminated after the Regional Transports were excluded from the PKP Group. To sum up, I would like to address the High House and all those concerned with a very simple question: Is there still public communication in our country and does anyone care about the comfort of travel, legal protection and the social interest of the participants in public transport? I would like to ask you to take an interest in this problem, because I believe that this problem does not only concern the previously mentioned Łukow and the district of Łukow. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u509	Thank you very much. Mr Waldemar Andzel, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-04-13-sejm-16-2.u510	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today, April 13th, the 65th anniversary of the death of Mieczysław Dziemiewicz pseudos. A hive, a soldier of the National Armed Forces and the National Military Union, a participant of the Polish independence anti-communist underground in northern Mazowsze. Mieczysław Dziemiewicz was born on 25 January 1925 in Zagrobach. At the age of 14, he graduated from universal school in Rosanie. During the occupation he participated in secret teaching courses in Maków Mazowiecki. At the same time, he worked in a German transport company. The Dziemiewiewicz family was involved in the underground independence activities carried out by the National Armed Forces. The two Mieczysław brothers, Roman and George, were part of this organization. That is why he also joined her during the German occupation. In the spring of 1945. Mieczysław Dziemieliewicz was incorporated into the 1st reserve regiment of the Polish Army in Warsaw, but from which, immediately after receiving news of the murder of his brother, he escaped. On the other hand, he joined the guerrilla branch of NSZ NZW. Marian Kraśniewski, who was in charge of the team. He participated in numerous actions against UB officers and MO stations. Initially, he served as a liaison between the district command and the district command. Two years later, he was promoted to senior sergeant. Among the most important actions carried out by the branch of Russia between June 1947 and August 1948 can be mentioned among others. Disarming the MO station in Barancze and Gąsocin, ambushing the UB under Gołymin and Nasielsk or fighting the KBW and UB operating group under Pniew Wielki. During that time, the branch of the Soviet Union closed down 12 UB, MO and ORMO officers and more than 20 security officers. Structures of the "Royal" have been developed within the framework of the so-called "Royal" structures. Object cases founded at the Police Police Command and codenamed "Devils' to the guerrilla unit under his command. Dozens of agents were involved. A group of former members of the NZW who murdered their former associates was assigned to the liquidation of the Soviet Union. However, even though they managed to make contact with the Russians, they did not decide to perform the task because of fear. Mieczysław Dziemiewicz died only in Szyszki on 13 April 1951, along with his soldier Bronisław Gniazdowski pseudod. Mazur, in the fight against 300 people operating group UBP and KBW. 8 August 1945 Mieczysław Dziemiewicz was awarded the Cross of Valiant. On 13 October 2007, the President of the Republic of Poland Lech Kaczyński posthumously awarded Mieczysław Dziemiewiewicz the Commander's Cross with the Star of the Order for the Rebirth of Poland. Russia was also awarded the Cross of the National Armed Action. Mieczysław Dziemieliewicz became the main character of the film devoted to the fate of the enchanted soldiers. The history of the Roja is better heard in the earth. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
